# Eric Schmidt (p0012)

## Review approach

This is a high-collision case. Use Schmidt's biography plus the SEC issuer biography identifying Hillspire as his family office. Executive/chairman/adviser records at Google/Alphabet are historical-role evidence; the biography dates those roles and no current-employment claim follows. Google software/developer-programs engineers are rejected despite the shared Google employer. SEC/lawyer, Glue Up CEO, utility, construction and medical groups receive explicit rejection when their specific occupation conflicts; Glue Up has a separately sourced Eric L. Schmidt CEO. ERIC M/L/J/A middle initials block the initial merge even when the name and state resemble the target. Self-employed and retired groups require a direct same-name, same-location, near-date anchor.

Examined 200 candidate evidence groups containing 1092 source records. Accepted 195 record links. Counts are not distinct gifts or dollar totals.

## Identity and collision sources

- [Eric Schmidt: About Eric](https://ericschmidt.com/bio/) — Identifies Eric Schmidt as Google's CEO/chairman from 2001 to 2011 and records later technology and philanthropic roles. Historical affiliation is not current employment. Observed 2026-09-20.
- [SEC issuer filing: Kenneth Goldman biography](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/2131350/000119312526262727/filename1.htm) — Identifies Hillspire LLC as Eric Schmidt's family office; records Goldman's presidency from 2017 to 2022. This supports the affiliation, not a specific current title for Schmidt. Observed 2026-09-20.
- [Collision: Glue Up CEO Eric L. Schmidt](https://www.glueup.com/legal/legal-notice) — Glue Up identifies its CEO as Eric L. Schmidt at its McLean, Virginia office, distinguishing the employer/initial context from the former Google leader. Observed 2026-09-20.

## Scope and unresolved work

Only the exact records and hashes listed below are covered. Known name variants and initials were retrieved across both installed cycles without a giving threshold. Unlisted surname typos, changed surnames and unitemized giving are not covered. Acceptance never changes reported employer or gift accounting. Initial decisions were prepared by Codex using explicitly documented person-specific criteria and then expanded to evidence groups; they are not human sign-offs.
